SUPERR RESEARCH READOUT: SUMMARY OF EVERY FILE, FOLDER BY FOLDER
======================================================================
This file describes every output in research_readout/. For each CSV it gives what the file holds, its shape, its source, its columns, and the data itself: all of it for small tables, the first rows for large ones. Markdown reports are included in full.
Project: a pressure-adjusted T20 rating of batters and bowlers (IPL 2022-2026, T20Is, Emerging / Rising Stars) from ball-by-ball data. Current model: 7 weighted components (Form removed), super overs included at knockout-level occasion, with the November 2026 fixes (ball-19 bug, weighted pressure index, wickets-down expectation cell, high-band floor 60, legal-ball clutch basis, 50/50 role blend for batters; see 12_model_fixes).

GLOSSARY OF COMMON COLUMNS AND TERMS
- ladder: IPL, T20I, or T20 (Emerging Teams / Rising Stars, weighted 0.5). Elo and expectations never mix ladders.
- legal ball: not a wide, no-ball or 'other' extra. Super overs ARE included (innings 3 and 4).
- n / legal_ball_n: legal-ball number within the innings. innings_ball_num: delivery number including illegal balls.
- phase: powerplay (first 30% of the innings), middle, death (last 20%). Every super-over ball is death.
- occasion: Final 3.0, Semi Final 2.0, Qualifier / Eliminator 1.5, Super 4 1.3, League 1.0, other bilateral 0.8, dead rubber 0.4. Super-over balls: max(stage, 1.5).
- competition: 0.7 for Emerging / Rising / Other T20, else 1.0.
- ball_bat / ball_bowl: ball difficulty (0..1) from phase, wickets down and required rate (rrr).
- shock_bat / shock_bowl: previous six balls (batter: wickets and dots; bowler: sixes and fours) plus the excess of wickets over xW (batter) or charged runs over xR (bowler); clipped to -0.5..1.
- delta / run_of_play: last 6 vs last 18 balls (batter: wickets; bowler: runs), scaled, clipped to -0.5..1. The ball-19 look-ahead bug (BUG-01) is fixed: before 18 balls the long window subtracts nothing.
- situation_bat / situation_bowl = occasion x competition x ball difficulty.
- pressure_bat / pressure_bowl = 0.60 x situation / SD(situation) + 0.25 x shock / SD(shock) + 0.15 x run of play / SD(run of play)  (PRESSURE_WEIGHTS in player_ratings.py; before the fixes it was the plain sum).
- band / band_bowl: zero (< p50), medium (p50..p80), high (>= p80). Batters are cut on all balls; bowlers separately within pace and within spin.
- x_runs (xR), x_runs_bowl, x_wicket (xW): expected bat runs, expected charged runs, expected wicket probability, using only earlier balls. The finest cell is ladder x phase x arm x wickets down (0-1, 2-3, 4-5, 6-7, 8+).
- e_bat = (runs - xR) - 4 x (out - xW); e_bowl = (xR_bowl - charged) + 4 x (wicket - xW). The ball value.
- n_eff: season-decayed (0.9 per year back from 2026), level-weighted legal balls. high_n_eff: the same in the high band.
- ranked: n_eff >= 120 (RANK_BALLS) and high_n_eff >= 60 (RANK_HIGH_BALLS; 30 before the fixes). rank: 1 = best, among ranked players only.
- components (z-scores capped at +/-3; bowlers pooled; a batter's z = 0.5 x z within top / middle / lower order + 0.5 x z across all batters, ROLE_BLEND): baseline 0.20, band_zero 0.10, band_medium 0.15, band_high 0.20, clutch 0.15, peers 0.10 (the same innings vs teammates and opposition), consistency 0.10. composite = the weighted sum. Bowler clutch compares high-band spell economy with career economy, both on legal balls.
- pre_fix_*: the model before the November 2026 fixes (research_readout/_scripts/reference/player_ratings_pre_fix.py), run in full on the same balls.
- legacy_*: the earlier 8-term model with Form 0.05 and consistency 0.05 (git 29956ed), scored on the pre-fix frame, so legacy vs pre-fix isolates the removal of Form.
- role: batters top / middle / lower order (modal batting position 1-3 / 4-6 / 7+); bowlers Pace / Spin.
- status: COMPUTED (calculated in this audit), EXISTING (already in the repository and reproduced), REQUIRES_DATA, REQUIRES_IMPLEMENTATION, NOT_APPLICABLE (Form).

README.md (full text):
# Research readout: data extraction and validation audit

This folder holds the audit of the SupeRR pressure-rating pipeline: extracted tables, newly computed statistics, charts and written findings. The readout scripts never write outside this folder. Every table here was produced by running `player_ratings.py`, as it stands in the working tree, in memory and reading its outputs.

**The model was fixed in November 2026.** The audit's findings were acted on at the user's request. `12_model_fixes/model_fixes.md` explains each change and `12_model_fixes/fix_before_after.csv` gives every before / after number.

| Finding | Change | Constant in `player_ratings.py` |
|---|---|---|
| BUG-01 ball-19 look-ahead | the 18-ball run-of-play window subtracts nothing before the innings has 18 balls | – |
| Pressure dominated by recent events | situation, shock and run of play are each divided by their own SD, then weighted 0.60 / 0.25 / 0.15 | `PRESSURE_WEIGHTS` |
| Wickets-fallen bias | the finest expectation cell adds wickets down (0-1, 2-3, 4-5, 6-7, 8+) | – |
| Small high-pressure samples | ranking floor on high-band n_eff raised from 30 to 60 | `RANK_HIGH_BALLS` |
| Bowler clutch delivery basis | career economy on legal balls, the same basis as the spell | – |
| Role normalisation | a batter's z-score is 0.5 within role + 0.5 across all batters | `ROLE_BLEND` |

"Before" or `pre_fix_*` everywhere in this folder means the model before these fixes. It is kept as `_scripts/reference/player_ratings_pre_fix.py` and run in full on the same balls.

**Super overs are included.** The data has 38 super-over deliveries (33 legal balls) in 6 matches, stored as innings 3 and 4.

- **Phase:** every super-over ball is a death ball.
- **Occasion:** max(stage occasion, 1.5), so a super over is never below knockout level, even in a dead rubber. A semi-final or final super over keeps 2.0 or 3.0.
- **Chase:** the second super over chases the first super over's total plus one.

`08_validation/ablation_summary.csv` compares the official run with the same pipeline run without super overs (`exclude_super_overs`).

## Version under audit

- **Official numbers use the current working tree.** It has seven weighted terms:

  | Term | Weight |
  |---|---|
  | Baseline | 0.20 |
  | Zero-pressure band | 0.10 |
  | Medium-pressure band | 0.15 |
  | High-pressure band | 0.20 |
  | Clutch | 0.15 |
  | Peers | 0.10 |
  | Consistency | 0.10 |

- **The brief describes eight terms** (Form 0.05, consistency 0.05). That is the version at git `29956ed`. Form was removed at the user's request, committed in `7af5382`, and is reported as **NOT_APPLICABLE**.
- **The 8-term scoring functions were re-run read-only from `29956ed`** on the pre-fix model's legal frame, so legacy vs pre-fix isolates the removal of Form. The commit is pinned in `run_pipeline.py`. It appears as `legacy_8_term_with_form_pre_fix_frame` and `legacy_*` columns.
- **The in-memory run reproduces the shipped `ratings/batters.csv` and `ratings/bowlers.csv` exactly**: max composite gap 0, identical ranks, 141 ranked batters and 143 ranked bowlers (216 and 226 before the fixes).

## Status labels

| Label | Meaning |
|---|---|
| COMPUTED | Calculated in this audit from the pipeline's frames |
| EXISTING | Already in the repository (code constant, export, or notebook) and reproduced here |
| REQUIRES_DATA | Cannot be computed from the supplied files (for example DLS par scores, external selections or awards) |
| REQUIRES_IMPLEMENTATION | Needs new modelling not done here (for example bootstrap rank intervals) |
| NOT_APPLICABLE | Not present in the current model (Form) |

Comparison runs (the pre-fix model, other pressure weights, other role blends, other floors) are alternatives. They never replace an official number.

## Layout

```
01_dataset/                 cleaning_summary, delivery_classification, dismissal_classification, cleaning_assumptions, dls_summary
02_pressure/                pressure_summary, pressure_percentiles, pressure_band_summary, phase_bowling_pressure, pressure_observations
                            + pressure_formula_as_executed.csv (formula with the executed scales and weights)
                            + super_over_pressure.csv (every legal super-over ball with its pressure terms)
03_components/              component_definitions, component_summary, batter_component_scores, bowler_component_scores, component_contributions
04_elo/                     elo_summary, batter_elo_history, bowler_elo_history, elo_stability
05_expectation_models/      xruns_calibration_deciles, xwickets_calibration_deciles, expectation_model_metrics, xruns_predictions, xwickets_predictions
                            + residual_by_wickets_fallen.csv (KI-03, current vs pre-fix)
06_shock/                   shock_parameters, shock_innings_timeline (IPL 2025 final, PBKS chase), shock_event_summary
07_rankings/                final_batter_rankings, final_bowler_rankings, top10_*, top20_*, composite_distribution
08_validation/              sanity_check_players, sanity_check_interpretations.md, ablation_summary, ablation_*_rank_changes, ablation_top_movers,
                            component_correlation_batters/bowlers, component_influence, role_coverage, role_ranking_representation, role_sample_distribution
                            + diagnostic_bowler_clutch_basis.csv (BUG-03)
                            + pressure_weight_sensitivity.csv, ranking_floor_sensitivity.csv
09_limitations/             limitations_register.csv, methodological_audit.md, cricketing_interpretations.md
10_charts/slide_XX_*/       matplotlib PNGs on a white background, one folder per slide
11_presentation_summary/    slide_by_slide_numbers.md (start here), key_findings.md, source_traceability.csv
12_model_fixes/             model_fixes.md, fix_before_after.csv
_scripts/                   the code that produced everything above (reference/ holds the pre-fix model)
_cache/                     pickled pipeline frames and per-script facts (git-ignored; rebuilt by run_pipeline.py)
```

## Reproduce

Run from `research_readout/_scripts/`:

```
python run_pipeline.py                 # player_ratings in memory, a no-super-over run, the pre-fix model, and the 8-term model from git 29956ed
python a_dataset_pressure.py           # 01, 02 (and the replication of the shock / run-of-play / pressure terms)
python b_components_elo_models.py      # 03, 04, 05 (includes the Elo step-size diagnostic)
python c_shock_rankings_validation.py  # 06, 07, 08 (pressure weight, floor and role-blend alternatives)
python d_charts.py                     # 10
python e_reports.py                    # 09, 11, 12, the interpretations markdown
python f_summary_txt.py                # RESEARCH_READOUT_SUMMARY.txt
```

The whole chain takes several minutes. The scripts write only inside `research_readout/`. Importing `player_ratings` lets Python refresh its own `__pycache__` bytecode; that is not source or data.

## Bugs and known issues

| ID | Issue | Status |
|---|---|---|
| BUG-01 | **Run-of-play look-ahead.** On legal ball 19 the 18-ball window subtracted the innings total (numpy index −1), which also inflated the scale used on every ball. | FIXED. Mean bowler run of play on ball 19 went from 1.000 to 0.251; the runs scale from 1.1072 to 0.5928. |
| BUG-02 | **Mislabelled export columns.** `exports/10_sample_innings_timeline.csv` columns `count_shock_*` hold the final shock (count plus excess). | Open; labels only. |
| BUG-03 | **Mismatched clutch basis.** Bowler clutch compared legal-ball spell economy with a career economy that included wide and no-ball runs. | FIXED. Both sides now use legal balls. |

Known issues KI-01 to KI-04 and methodological limitations M-01 to M-19 are in `09_limitations/limitations_register.csv`. KI-03 (no wickets-fallen feature) is fixed. Three limitations are new with the fixes: M-17 (the bowler high band now leans to the powerplay), M-18 (role blend is a modelling choice) and M-19 (pressure weights are a judgement). The expectation models still discriminate weakly between single balls (M-07, open).

## Interpretation (analyst reading of the numbers above)

- **The small-sample bowlers who led the old table.** Humphreys (before 2) has a high-band n_eff of 43.0 and is not ranked (high_n_eff 43.0 < 60); Bosch (before 1, high n_eff 44.4) is not ranked. Bumrah, on 1708 legal balls, ranks 5 (before 8). The floor of 60 high-band balls and the new pressure index both work against thin pressure samples.
- **Sooryavanshi** has a high-band n_eff of 33.6, below the new floor of 60, so he is not ranked (baseline z +2.53; before the fixes 7).
- **Established top-order batters**: Buttler 5, Kohli 28 (before 34 and 30). Rossouw was 1 before the fixes on a high-band n_eff of 55.6; under the new pressure index he has 22.9 and is not ranked. The pooled half of the z-score and a situation-led high band weigh sustained output more than a short run of post-wicket innings.
- **Rashid Khan (bowler)** ranks 39 (before 117); clutch -0.59, high band -0.84. Read his high-band terms with M-17 in mind: the high band now leans to the powerplay, where he rarely bowls.

--- 09_limitations/cricketing_interpretations.md
WHAT IT HAS: What the results mean in cricket terms (marked as interpretation).
FULL TEXT:
# Cricketing interpretations

These readings rest on the computed numbers. Each one says which number it rests on. They describe what the model measures, not claims about players beyond the data.

1. **Batting pressure is now mostly the match situation.** The batter high band covers 58.9% of death balls, 86.4% of final balls and 10.9% of powerplay balls. Recent wickets still matter: 75.6% of high-band balls follow one, against 27.5% of all balls. In the high band, strike rate is 132.8 against 142.6 in the zero band, and the striker-out rate 6.99% against 4.88%.
2. **Bowling pressure now leans to the new ball.** 72.1% of pace high-band balls are in the powerplay and 10.5% at the death (M-17). Economy on legal balls from the zero to the high band: pace 8.46 → 9.25, spin 7.26 → 8.68. A death specialist's high-band sample is now smaller than an opening bowler's.
3. **Spin carries the middle overs.** 60.1% of spin high-band balls are in the middle overs and 35.5% in the powerplay.
4. **The batting top 10 by role** is 4/5/1 (top/middle/lower). Lower-order ranked batters have 55.8% of their balls in the high band, against 12.3% for top-order batters: death overs and wickets down are now the main route into the band.
5. **The role mix is a modelling choice.** Top 20 (top/middle/lower): role-only 4/12/4, 50/50 blend 6/10/4, pooled 8/9/3. Before the fixes the same switch moved it from 6/7/7 to 14/6/0. Batters are compared partly within their batting role because the roles are different jobs. An opener faces the new ball with no wickets down and is judged on building an innings; a finisher arrives at the death, often after a collapse, and is judged on scoring fast against set bowlers. A strike rate or average is not comparable across those jobs without some adjustment. Pooling every batter rewards the role whose numbers are easiest to post. A role-only z-score assumes each role is equally deep in talent. Neither is a finding about cricket. The 50/50 blend (ROLE_BLEND = 0.5) is a stated compromise between the two.
6. **Batting ranks rest mostly on** baseline 29.1%, high pressure 27.2%, clutch 21.2%, peers 11.5% (variance shares).
7. **Bowling ranks rest mostly on** high pressure 25.9%, clutch 20.9%, baseline 19.9%, peers 13.5%. Baseline-only and full rankings share 10 of the top 20.
8. **Tail situations no longer distort ball value much.** At 8–9 down, batters average -0.088 runs-equivalent per ball (before -0.421), and bowlers facing them 0.057 (before 0.391).
9. **The 2025 final chase.** PBKS's 7 wickets fell at legal balls 30, 51, 58, 73, 98, 100, 104. Batter pressure peaks at 8.35 on ball 99. 120 of the 120 balls are batter-high (a final at occasion 3.0).

BUG-01,BUG,Run-of-play term read the whole innings on legal ball 19 (FIXED),"In the pre-fix _shock_and_delta the 18-ball window was wicket_sum[end] - wicket_sum[end-18]. At position 18 (legal ball 19) end-18 = -1, and numpy index -1 is the LAST element, so the window subtracted the innings total: ball 19 used later balls, and the extreme values inflated the SD that scales every other ball. The window now subtracts 0 when end-18 < 0.","Pre-fix behaviour rebuilt and checked against the pre-fix model's own columns (max gap 0.0). M-01,METHODOLOGICAL,"Recent events still lift the high band, but no longer define it (PARTLY FIXED)","Pressure was the raw sum of situation, shock and run of play. Shock and run of play have much larger spreads, so the high band was almost entirely the six balls after a wicket (batters) or a boundary (bowlers). Each term is now divided by its own SD and weighted: 0.60 situation + 0.25 shock + 0.15 run of play.","Batter high-band balls within six balls after a wicket: 99.9% before, 75.6% now (base rate 27.5%). Finals in the batter high band: 26.1% -> 86.4%.",Medium (interpretation),every 'pressure' claim,PARTLY FIXED,Recent events are still over-represented by design (shock 0.25 + run of play 0.15 of the weight). --- 09_limitations/methodological_audit.md
WHAT IT HAS: What the code actually executes, section by section (version, data, pressure, Elo, expectations, components, ablation, issues), with numbers.
FULL TEXT:
# Methodological audit

This audit describes what the code in this repository executes, checked against the data. Where a number is given, it comes from a file in `research_readout/` (see `11_presentation_summary/source_traceability.csv`). The fixes made in November 2026 are listed in section 0 and measured in `12_model_fixes/`.

## 0. Version under audit

- `player_ratings.py`: **seven** weighted terms. Baseline 0.20, zero band 0.10, medium band 0.15, high band 0.20, clutch 0.15, peers 0.10, consistency 0.10. They sum to 1.00.
- Changes in this round, all in `player_ratings.py`:
  - BUG-01: the 18-ball run-of-play window no longer reads the innings total on legal ball 19.
  - Pressure: each term over its own SD, weighted 0.60 situation + 0.25 shock + 0.15 run of play (`PRESSURE_WEIGHTS`). Before, the three terms were added raw.
  - Expectation cells: the full cell adds wickets down (0-1, 2-3, 4-5, 6-7, 8+).
  - Batter z-scores: 0.5 within role + 0.5 against all batters (`ROLE_BLEND`). Before, within role only.
  - Ranking floor: high-band n_eff ≥ 60 (`RANK_HIGH_BALLS`, was 30); n_eff ≥ 120 unchanged.
  - Bowler clutch compares legal-ball spell economy with legal-ball career economy.
- The model before these fixes is kept at `research_readout/_scripts/reference/player_ratings_pre_fix.py` and run in full for every before/after number. The 8-term version with Form (git `29956ed`) is scored on the pre-fix frame, so legacy vs pre-fix isolates the Form change.
- Super overs are included: 38 deliveries, 33 legal balls, 6 matches, each ball a death ball at occasion max(stage, 1.5).
- Reproduction: the in-memory run reproduces the shipped `ratings/batters.csv` ranks: True. Ranked: 141 batters, 143 bowlers.

## 1. Data and cleaning

- 200,999 deliveries; none are deleted in cleaning. Duplicate delivery keys: 0.
- Excluded from ball value: 8,554 illegal deliveries (7,714 wides, 576 no-balls, 264 'other').
- Used: **192,445 legal balls** in 867 matches, 2022-01-22 to 2026-11-01. IPL 83,755 balls in 367 matches; T20I 101,960 in 470; T20 (Emerging/Rising) 6,730 in 30.
- Reduced matches: 34. Reduced chases with no supplied target get no required rate (2 matches). DLS par scores are not in the data (REQUIRES_DATA).

## 2. Pressure as executed

```
pressure = 0.60 x situation / sd(situation) + 0.25 x shock / sd(shock) + 0.15 x run_of_play / sd(run_of_play)
situation = occasion x competition x ball difficulty
```
  Computed separately for the batter and the bowler side; the SDs are population SDs over all legal balls (`02_pressure/pressure_formula_as_executed.csv`).
- Occasion: Final 3.0, Semi 2.0, Qualifier/Eliminator 1.5, Super 4 1.3, League 1.0, other bilateral 0.8, dead rubber 0.4 (40 matches). A super-over ball takes max(stage occasion, 1.5). Competition: 0.7 for Emerging/Rising, otherwise 1.0.
- Ball difficulty (clipped 0..1). Batter: phase term (PP 0.25, middle 0.10, death 0.40) + wickets term (2–4 down 0.15, 5+ down 0.30) + chase-rate term (RRR ≥ 8 adds 0.15, ≥ 12 adds 0.30). Bowler: phase term (PP 0.32, middle 0.24; death 0.32 to a top-7 batter, otherwise 0.14) + wickets term (−0.10 or −0.25) + the same rate term.
- Shock: a count part over the previous six legal balls (batter 0.33 per wicket + 0.05 per dot; bowler 0.33 per six + 0.12 per four) plus an excess part (batter: bowler wickets − xW; bowler: charged − xR), scaled by SDs of 0.5104 and 4.3778, clipped −0.5..1.
- Run of play: last 6 vs last 18 balls (wickets per ball for batters, bat runs per ball for bowlers), scaled by SDs of 0.0733 and 0.5928 (pre-fix, with BUG-01: 0.0830 and 1.1072), clipped −0.5..1.
- Replication: the readout recomputes every term independently; max gap against the pipeline is 6.2e-15.
- Final cut-offs: batter p50 0.820, p80 1.523. Pace p50 1.116, p80 1.688. Spin p50 0.932, p80 1.529.
- Variance shares of batter pressure (now / before): situation 68.7% / 7.1%, shock 20.8% / 51.8%, run of play 10.4% / 41.1%. Bowlers: 71.7% / 3.1%, 18.8% / 59.3%, 9.5% / 37.6%.
- **What the high band captures now.** 75.6% of batter high-band balls follow a wicket within six balls (before 99.9%; base rate 27.5%). 58.9% of death balls and 86.4% of final balls are batter-high. For bowlers the high band leans to the powerplay (M-17).

## 3. Expectation models and ball value

- Cells are ladder → phase → phase × arm → phase × arm × wickets down, each pulled toward the coarser level with 30 balls. Priors 1.0 run and 0.05 wickets. Every prediction uses only balls already bowled.
- The Elo gap is 0.15 × (batter − bowler)/400 + 0.05 × (side gap)/400 + 0.10 × (ground − 1500)/400. xR = cell mean × exp(gap), capped 0..6; xW = logistic(logit(cell rate) − gap), capped 0.002..0.40.
- Batter ball value = (bat runs − xR) − 4 × (striker out − xW). Bowler ball value = (xR_bowl − charged) + 4 × (bowler wicket − xW).
- xR: MAE 1.2986 (before 1.3043; constant 1.3282), RMSE 1.7284, OLS slope 0.922, R² 0.0159 (before 0.0133).
- xW against bowler wickets: AUC 0.5601 (before 0.5597), Brier 0.05026 vs 0.05041 constant, logistic slope 0.729. Discrimination stays weak (M-07).
- Runs actual minus expected at 8–9 down: -0.044 per ball (before -0.345); at 0–1 down 0.048 (before 0.084).

## 4. Elo

- Ratings start at 1500 per (player, ladder). Steps 4 / 16 / 20, not halved. Final raw ratings: batters 1043.2–2500.4 (SD 124.8); bowlers 1062.5–2082.6 (SD 113.2).
- Step-size sensitivity: composite Spearman 0.996 / 0.994 for batters, 0.994 / 0.990 for bowlers.

## 5. Components and composite

- **Baseline** (weight 0.20). Ranked batters: SD 0.901, variance share 29.1%. Ranked bowlers: SD 0.690, variance share 19.9%.
- **Zero pressure** (weight 0.10). Ranked batters: SD 0.928, variance share 4.3%. Ranked bowlers: SD 0.729, variance share 3.9%.
- **Medium pressure** (weight 0.15). Ranked batters: SD 0.950, variance share 10.2%. Ranked bowlers: SD 0.782, variance share 8.0%.
- **High pressure** (weight 0.20). Ranked batters: SD 0.952, variance share 27.2%. Ranked bowlers: SD 0.868, variance share 25.9%.
- **Clutch** (weight 0.15). Ranked batters: SD 1.213, variance share 21.2%. Ranked bowlers: SD 1.186, variance share 20.9%.
- **Peers** (weight 0.10). Ranked batters: SD 0.914, variance share 11.5%. Ranked bowlers: SD 0.981, variance share 13.5%.
- **Consistency** (weight 0.10). Ranked batters: SD 1.002, variance share -3.5%. Ranked bowlers: SD 0.900, variance share 8.0%.
- Batter z-scores: 0.5 × z within top/middle/lower order + 0.5 × z across all batters. Bowlers are z-scored across all bowlers; their bands are cut within pace and within spin.
- Why the role blend: Batters are compared partly within their batting role because the roles are different jobs. An opener faces the new ball with no wickets down and is judged on building an innings; a finisher arrives at the death, often after a collapse, and is judged on scoring fast against set bowlers. A strike rate or average is not comparable across those jobs without some adjustment. Pooling every batter rewards the role whose numbers are easiest to post. A role-only z-score assumes each role is equally deep in talent. Neither is a finding about cricket. The 50/50 blend (ROLE_BLEND = 0.5) is a stated compromise between the two.
- Eligibility: n_eff ≥ 120 and high-band n_eff ≥ 60, both decay- and level-weighted.

## 6. Robustness

- Batters, compared with the official ranking:
  - the model before the fixes: Spearman 0.845, top-20 overlap 9
  - baseline only: Spearman 0.782, top-20 overlap 10
  - equal weights: Spearman 0.979
  - raw-sum pressure with the bug fixed: Spearman 0.836, top-20 overlap 9
  - pressure_weights_40_35_25: Spearman 0.903, top-20 overlap 11
  - pressure_weights_50_30_20: Spearman 0.959, top-20 overlap 14
  - pressure_weights_70_20_10: Spearman 0.947, top-20 overlap 17
  - pressure_weights_equal_thirds: Spearman 0.861, top-20 overlap 8
  - role-only z: Spearman 0.991, top-20 overlap 18; pooled z: 0.990, 17
  - super overs excluded: Spearman 0.9999
- Bowlers, compared with the official ranking:
  - the model before the fixes: Spearman 0.785, top-20 overlap 12
  - baseline only: Spearman 0.662, top-20 overlap 10
  - equal weights: Spearman 0.981
  - raw-sum pressure with the bug fixed: Spearman 0.722, top-20 overlap 12
  - pressure_weights_40_35_25: Spearman 0.862, top-20 overlap 13
  - pressure_weights_50_30_20: Spearman 0.947, top-20 overlap 16
  - pressure_weights_70_20_10: Spearman 0.953, top-20 overlap 18
  - pressure_weights_equal_thirds: Spearman 0.838, top-20 overlap 14
  - super overs excluded: Spearman 0.9997

## 7. Issues

See `limitations_register.csv`. Fixed this round: BUG-01, BUG-03, KI-03, M-06; partly fixed: M-01. Open: BUG-02, KI-02, KI-04, M-07. New: M-17 (bowler high band leans to the powerplay). Modelling choices stated as such: M-18 (role blend), M-19 (pressure weights).

--- 11_presentation_summary/key_findings.md
WHAT IT HAS: The 12 most important findings, in priority order, with source files.
FULL TEXT:
# Key findings

Ordered by importance for the presentation. Every number links to a file in this pack.

1. **Six fixes went in, and the tables moved a lot.** Against the pre-fix model: batters Spearman 0.845, top-20 overlap 9/20; bowlers 0.785, 12/20. *(12_model_fixes)*
2. **The top of each table.** Batters: 1. Tim David, 2. Phil Salt, 3. Rinku Singh, 4. Donovan Ferreira, 5. Jos Buttler. Bowlers: 1. Mitchell Santner, 2. Shaheen Afridi, 3. Bhuvneshwar Kumar, 4. Abrar Ahmed, 5. Jasprit Bumrah. *(07_rankings)*
3. **'High pressure' now mostly means the match situation.** Situation explains 68.7% of batter pressure variance (before 7.1%). Recent wickets still lift the band: 75.6% of batter high-band balls follow one (before 99.9%; base rate 27.5%). Finals are 86.4% high (before 26.1%). *(02_pressure, 06_shock)*
4. **New side effect: the bowler high band leans to the powerplay.** 72.1% of pace high-band balls are in the powerplay and 10.5% at the death (before 35.5% and 28.4%). It comes from the bowler ball-difficulty table, which lowers death difficulty when wickets are down. *(M-17)*
5. **BUG-01 is fixed.** Ball 19 run of play: bowlers 1.000 → 0.251; the runs scale 1.1072 → 0.5928. *(09_limitations BUG-01)*
6. **The wickets-down cell removes most of the tail bias.** Runs above expectation at 8–9 down -0.345 → -0.044 per ball. Discrimination barely improves: xR R² 0.0133 → 0.0159, MAE 1.2986 vs 1.3282 constant, xW AUC 0.5601. *(05_expectation_models)*
7. **No thin pressure samples at the top.** The high-band floor is 60. Top-10 bowlers below 60: 4 → 0; the smallest top-10 bowler sample is 87.0. Ranked pools shrink: 141 batters, 143 bowlers. *(08_validation/ranking_floor_sensitivity.csv)*
8. **Role normalisation is a stated choice.** Top-20 batting mix: role-only 4/12/4, official 50/50 blend 6/10/4, pooled 8/9/3 (before the fixes: 6/7/7 vs 14/6/0). *(M-18, 08_validation/role_ranking_representation.csv)*
9. **Clutch now compares like with like.** On the current spells, 47.0% would beat the bowler's own rate on the old basis, 43.5% on legal balls. *(BUG-03)*
10. **The pressure layer still changes the ranking a lot.** Baseline-only vs full: 10/20 batters, 10/20 bowlers in common. Other pressure weights: batters Spearman 0.903–0.959, bowlers 0.862–0.953. *(08_validation/ablation_summary.csv, pressure_weight_sensitivity.csv)*
11. **Super overs now reach the high band** (90.9% batter, 100.0% bowler; none before) and still barely move the tables (Spearman 0.9999 / 0.9997 vs excluding them). *(02_pressure/super_over_pressure.csv)*
12. **Form removal, isolated on the pre-fix frame, mattered little**: Spearman 0.989 (batters), 0.991 (bowlers). Elo stays inside its bounds, steps not halved. *(08_validation, 04_elo)*

# Slide-by-slide numbers

One section per slide. Status tags: **[C]** COMPUTED here, **[E]** EXISTING in the repository and reproduced, **[RD]** REQUIRES_DATA, **[RI]** REQUIRES_IMPLEMENTATION, **[NA]** NOT_APPLICABLE. ## Slide 1: Objective and scope

- Rank T20 batters and bowlers on how they perform under pressure, using 192,445 legal balls from 2022-01-22 to 2026-11-01. [C]
- 141 batters and 143 bowlers meet the ranking floor (n_eff ≥ 120 and high-band n_eff ≥ 60). Before the fixes: 216 and 226 at a floor of 30. [C]

## Slide 3: The pressure index

- pressure = 0.60 situation + 0.25 shock + 0.15 run of play, each term over its own SD; situation = occasion × competition × ball difficulty (`02_pressure/pressure_formula_as_executed.csv`). Before the fix the three terms were added raw. [E]

## Slide 5: Expected runs and wickets (ball value)

- Ball value: batter = (runs − xR) − 4 × (out − xW); bowler = (xR − charged) + 4 × (wicket − xW). Cells now include wickets down. Predictions use only earlier balls. [E]
| scope | model | target | mean pred | mean actual | MAE | MAE constant | R² | OLS slope | logit slope | AUC | Brier |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| all | xR | bat_runs | 1.375 | 1.394 | 1.299 | 1.328 | 0.016 | 0.922 | – | – | – |
| all | xW | bowler_wicket | 0.051 | 0.053 | 0.099 | 0.101 | – | – | 0.729 | 0.560 | 0.050 |
| all | xW | striker_out | 0.051 | 0.055 | 0.100 | 0.104 | – | – | 0.798 | 0.566 | 0.052 |
| all_pre_fix_model | xR | bat_runs | 1.381 | 1.394 | 1.304 | 1.328 | 0.013 | 0.985 | – | – | – |
| all_pre_fix_model | xW | bowler_wicket | 0.051 | 0.053 | 0.099 | 0.101 | – | – | 0.811 | 0.560 | 0.050 |
| all_pre_fix_model | xW | striker_out | 0.051 | 0.055 | 0.100 | 0.104 | – | – | 0.891 | 0.566 | 0.052 |

|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

| batter | top order | 234 | 186 | 36 | 0.255 | 0 | 79 |
| bowler | Pace | 367 | 286 | 90 | 0.629 | 0 | 76 |
| bowler | Spin | 280 | 187 | 53 | 0.371 | 0 | 42 |
- Batting top 20 (top/middle/lower):
  - now: role-only z 4/12/4; **official 50/50 blend 6/10/4**; pooled z 8/9/3.
  - before the fixes: role-only 6/7/7; pooled 14/6/0. Legacy 8-term: role-only 6/7/7, pooled 12/5/3. [C]
- Why roles at all: Batters are compared partly within their batting role because the roles are different jobs. An opener faces the new ball with no wickets down and is judged on building an innings; a finisher arrives at the death, often after a collapse, and is judged on scoring fast against set bowlers. A strike rate or average is not comparable across those jobs without some adjustment. Pooling every batter rewards the role whose numbers are easiest to post. A role-only z-score assumes each role is equally deep in talent. Neither is a finding about cricket. The 50/50 blend (ROLE_BLEND = 0.5) is a stated compromise between the two.
- Charts: `10_charts/slide_14_roles/`.

## Slide 15: Limitations, fixes and bugs

- **BUG-01 Run-of-play term read the whole innings on legal ball 19 (FIXED)** [FIXED]. Pre-fix behaviour rebuilt and checked against the pre-fix model's own columns (max gap 0.0). Ball 19 mean run of play: batter 0.983 before, 0.076 now; bowler 1.000 before, 0.251 now. Scales: runs 1.1072 before, 0.5928 now; wickets 0.0830 before, 0.0733 now. Ball-19 bowler high-band share 54.9% before, 45.0% now (neighbouring balls 18 and 20 sit at a similar level under the new weights).
- **BUG-02 Deck export 10 labels final shock as 'count_shock'** [VERIFIED (open)]. exports/10_sample_innings_timeline.csv count_shock_bat equals final shock: True; equals count part: False.
- **BUG-03 Bowler clutch compared spell economy without extras to career economy with extras (FIXED)** [FIXED]. Ranked bowlers: career economy with extras minus legal-ball economy = 0.355 runs/over on average. On the current high-band spells, 47.0% would beat the bowler's own rate on the old basis against 43.5% on the legal-ball basis; mean own gap -0.162 vs -0.268 scale units. Comparison economy equals legal-ball economy to 0.0.
- **KI-01 Form term used a full-career bar (look-ahead)** [VERIFIED (legacy); NOT_APPLICABLE (current)]. Source at git 29956ed: bar = float(career.loc[player]). Form was removed earlier (7 terms, consistency 0.10).
- **KI-02 xWicket is trained on bowler wickets but charged against every striker dismissal** [VERIFIED (open)]. Legal balls: striker_out 10,567 vs bowler_wicket 10,247; striker run outs 315; non-striker dismissals 266. Mean xW 5.14% vs striker-out rate 5.49%. Mean wicket part of ball value: batter -0.0140, bowler 0.0074 per ball.
- **KI-03 No wickets-fallen feature in the expectation model (FIXED)** [FIXED]. Runs actual minus expected per ball, before -> now: 0-1 down 0.084 -> 0.048; 2-3 0.018 -> -0.001; 4-5 -0.011 -> 0.012; 6-7 -0.104 -> 0.007; 8-9 -0.345 -> -0.044. Mean bowler ball value at 8-9 down 0.391 -> 0.057.
- **KI-04 Wides and no-balls never enter the ball value or Elo** [VERIFIED (open)]. 11,278 of 279,563 conceded runs (4.0%) are on illegal deliveries; 25 bowler wickets (stumpings off wides) are credited in baseline only.
- **M-01 Recent events still lift the high band, but no longer define it (PARTLY FIXED)** [PARTLY FIXED]. Batter high-band balls within six balls after a wicket: 99.9% before, 75.6% now (base rate 27.5%). Bowler high-band balls after a four or six in the last six: 100.0% before, 86.7% now (base rate 63.6%). Situation share of pressure variance: batters 7.1% -> 68.7%, bowlers 3.1% -> 71.7%. Finals in the batter high band: 26.1% -> 86.4%.
- **M-06 Small high-pressure samples at the top of the bowling table (FIXED)** [FIXED]. Before the fix, 4 of the top-10 bowlers had high n_eff < 60 (Matthew Humphreys 31.3, Haider Ali 58.5, Imad Wasim 38.5, Dwayne Bravo 39.4). Now 0; the smallest high n_eff in the bowling top 10 is 87.0 and in the top 20 78.7. Ranked pools: batters 216 -> 141, bowlers 226 -> 143.
- **M-07 Expectation models discriminate weakly (OPEN)** [VERIFIED (open)]. xR MAE 1.3043 -> 1.2986 against 1.3282 for a constant; R^2 0.0133 -> 0.0159. xW AUC 0.5597 -> 0.5601; calibration slope 0.922 (xR), logistic 0.729 (xW).
- **M-17 Bowler high band moved toward the powerplay (NEW, side effect of the pressure fix)** [VERIFIED (open)]. Share of pace high-band balls by phase, before -> now: powerplay 35.5% -> 72.1%, middle 36.2% -> 17.4%, death 28.4% -> 10.5%. Pace death balls in the high band: 23.9% -> 8.8%.
- **M-18 Batters are z-scored half within role, half against all batters** [MODELLING CHOICE]. Top-20 batting role mix (top/middle/lower): role-only z 4/12/4, official 50/50 blend 6/10/4, pooled z 8/9/3. Before the fixes: role-only 6/7/7, pooled 14/6/0. Rank agreement with the official blend: role-only Spearman 0.991, pooled 0.990.
- **M-19 Pressure weights 0.60/0.25/0.15 are a judgement** [MODELLING CHOICE]. pre_fix_model (raw sum, ball-19 bug): batter after-wicket 99.9%, finals high 26.1%, pace death 28.4%, Spearman bat 0.845 / bowl 0.785; official: batter after-wicket 75.6%, finals high 86.4%, pace death 10.5%; pressure_weights_40_35_25: batter after-wicket 96.3%, finals high 54.0%, pace death 18.3%, Spearman bat 0.903 / bowl 0.862; pressure_weights_50_30_20: batter after-wicket 88.3%, finals high 63.5%, pace death 14.3%, Spearman bat 0.959 / bowl 0.947; pressure_weights_70_20_10: batter after-wicket 63.1%, finals high 86.4%, pace death 10.8%, Spearman bat 0.947 / bowl 0.953; pressure_weights_equal_thirds: batter after-wicket 98.4%, finals high 47.2%, pace death 20.1%, Spearman bat 0.861 / bowl 0.838; pressure_raw_sum_bug_fixed: batter after-wicket 99.9%, finals high 26.0%, pace death 29.9%, Spearman bat 0.836 / bowl 0.722
- Full register: `09_limitations/limitations_register.csv`; before/after: `12_model_fixes/fix_before_after.csv`. Chart: `10_charts/slide_15_limitations/`.

--- 12_model_fixes/model_fixes.md
WHAT IT HAS: What was changed, why, what moved, the rationale for normalising batters partly within roles, and how the pressure weights were chosen.
FULL TEXT:
# Model fixes (November 2026)

What was changed, why, and what moved. Before = the pre-fix model (`_scripts/reference/player_ratings_pre_fix.py`), run in full on the same balls. Numbers: `fix_before_after.csv`.

## Changes

| finding | change | constant |
|---|---|---|
| BUG-01 ball-19 look-ahead | the 18-ball window subtracts 0 before the innings has 18 balls | – |
| Pressure dominated by recent events | each term over its own SD, then weighted 0.60 / 0.25 / 0.15 | `PRESSURE_WEIGHTS` |
| Wickets-fallen bias | full expectation cell = ladder x phase x arm x wickets down (0-1, 2-3, 4-5, 6-7, 8+) | – |
| Small high-pressure samples | high-band n_eff floor 30 → 60 | `RANK_HIGH_BALLS` |
| Bowler clutch delivery basis | career economy on legal balls, same as the spell | – |
| Role normalisation | batter z = 0.5 within role + 0.5 pooled | `ROLE_BLEND` |

## What moved

- **Rankings.** Against the pre-fix model: batters Spearman 0.845, top-20 overlap 9/20; bowlers 0.785, 12/20. The pressure weighting alone is a change of that size: keeping every other fix but going back to raw-sum pressure (bug fixed) gives Spearman 0.836 for batters and 0.722 for bowlers against the official ranking.
- **Pressure.** Batter high-band balls after a recent wicket 99.9% → 75.6% (base rate 27.5%); bowler high-band balls after a boundary 100.0% → 86.7% (base rate 63.6%). Finals in the batter high band 26.1% → 86.4%.
- **Side effect (M-17).** Pace high-band balls in the powerplay 35.5% → 72.1%, at the death 28.4% → 10.5%. This follows from the existing bowler ball-difficulty table and is not tuned here.
- **Expectations.** The 8-9 down residual -0.345 → -0.044 runs per ball. Discrimination barely moves: xR MAE 1.3043 → 1.2986 (constant 1.3282), R² 0.0133 → 0.0159, xW AUC 0.5597 → 0.5601. Single-ball T20 outcomes are mostly noise; this limitation stays open (M-07).
- **Samples.** Top-10 bowlers with high n_eff < 60: 4 → 0. Ranked pools: batters 216 → 141, bowlers 226 → 143.
- **Clutch.** On the current high-band spells, 47.0% would beat their own rate on the old mixed basis; 43.5% do on the legal-ball basis.
- **Roles.** Top-20 batting mix (top/middle/lower): role-only 4/12/4, official blend 6/10/4, pooled 8/9/3; before the fixes role-only 6/7/7 and pooled 14/6/0.

## Why batters are normalised within roles (partly)

Batters are compared partly within their batting role because the roles are different jobs. An opener faces the new ball with no wickets down and is judged on building an innings; a finisher arrives at the death, often after a collapse, and is judged on scoring fast against set bowlers. A strike rate or average is not comparable across those jobs without some adjustment. Pooling every batter rewards the role whose numbers are easiest to post. A role-only z-score assumes each role is equally deep in talent. Neither is a finding about cricket. The 50/50 blend (ROLE_BLEND = 0.5) is a stated compromise between the two.

The evidence that this is a choice and not a discovery: the official ranking agrees with the role-only ranking at Spearman 0.991 and with the pooled ranking at 0.990, but the top-20 role mix changes visibly between them. After the wickets-down fix the role-only and pooled tables are much closer than before, which suggests part of what role normalisation was compensating for was the tail-end expectation bias.

## Pressure weights

The weights are a judgement, chosen from the table in `08_validation/pressure_weight_sensitivity.csv`. Lower situation weights keep the high band tied to recent events; higher ones push death bowling further out of the bowler high band. Chart: `10_charts/slide_04_high_pressure_meaning/pressure_weight_sensitivity.png`.
